// I5: what a bug fix lacks is refused, naming it.

const BUG_FIX_INTENT_NEEDS_ITS_PARTS: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.bug-fix-intent-needs-its-parts',
  title: 'a bug-fix intent missing or blank in any of steps, actual, or expected is refused at admission, naming the part; a bug fix given free text, or a run with no template given a bug-fix intent, is refused; the Vault refuses to record a bug-fix intent missing a part',
  timeoutMs: 120_000,
  run: async () => {
    const { admitRun } = await api();
    await withLine('r16-parts-', async (rig) => {
      const r = await drafting(rig);
      for (const part of ['steps', 'actual', 'expected'] as const) {
        const { [part]: _dropped, ...rest } = BUG_INTENT;
        names(problemsOf(await admitRun(await bugRequest(r, { intent: rest as never })), `a bug fix with no ${part}`), `intent.${part}`, 'missing', `a bug fix with no ${part}`);
        names(problemsOf(await admitRun(await bugRequest(r, { intent: { ...BUG_INTENT, [part]: '  ' } })), `a bug fix with a blank ${part}`), `intent.${part}`, 'missing', `a bug fix with a blank ${part}`);
      }
      names(problemsOf(await admitRun(await bugRequest(r, { intent: { text: 'fix the hello bug', from: 'principal' } })), 'a bug fix given free text'), 'intent', 'not-an-intent', 'a bug fix given free text');
      names(problemsOf(await admitRun(await r.request({ artifacts: artifactsFor(true, true), intent: BUG_INTENT })), 'a bug-fix intent with no template'), 'intent', 'not-applicable', 'a bug-fix intent with no template');
      const vault = (await r.components()).vault;
      await vault.recordIntent({ runId: rig.runId, template: 'bug-fix', steps: BUG_INTENT.steps, actual: '', expected: BUG_INTENT.expected, from: 'principal', collectedBy: 'runtime' }).then(
        () => { throw new Error('R16: the Vault recorded a bug-fix intent with a blank part'); },
        (error: unknown) => { if (!String(error).includes('part')) throw error; },
      );
    });
  },
});

const BUG_FIX_TESTS_NEED_A_REPRODUCTION: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.bug-fix-tests-need-a-reproduction',
  title: 'a bug fix\'s test draft with no required check declaring reproduces, or with one declaring it beside onBase passes, is sent back naming it; the approved draft\'s reproducing check fails on the base; a test draft declaring reproduces in a run that is not a bug fix is sent back as not applicable',
  timeoutMs: 240_000,
  run: async () => {
    await withLine('r16-repro-', async (rig) => {
      const r = await drafting(rig, {
        spec: (_, id) => bugSpec(id),
        tests: (attempt, id) => attempt === 1 ? bugTests(id, { reproduces: undefined }) : attempt === 2 ? bugTests(id, { onBase: 'passes', id: 'guard' }) : bugTests(id),
      });
      awaiting(await admitAndDrive(r, await bugRequest(r)), 'spec:1', 'a bug fix\'s spec');
      const spec = lastAttempt(await rig.state(), 'spec');
      awaiting(await approveAndResume(rig, r, 'spec:1', spec.sha256 ?? ''), 'test-design:1', 'the bug fix\'s third test draft');
      const [one, two, three] = (await rig.state()).drafts['test-design']?.attempts ?? [];
      const manifest = `.drafts/${rig.runId}/acceptance.json`;
      const back = (a: typeof one): ReadonlyArray<{ path: string; code: string }> => (a?.sentBack?.by === 'runtime' ? a.sentBack.problems : []);
      if (!back(one).some((p) => p.path === `${manifest}.checks` && p.code === 'no-reproduction')) throw new Error(`I5: a test draft with no reproduction went back for ${JSON.stringify(one?.sentBack)}`);
      if (!back(two).some((p) => p.code === 'not-fails')) throw new Error(`I5: a reproduction declared to pass on the base went back for ${JSON.stringify(two?.sentBack)}`);
      if (three?.baseRun == null) throw new Error('R16: the good test draft was not run on the base');
      const base = await readRecord<{ checks: Array<{ checkId: string; exitCode: number | null }> }>((await r.components()).vault, three.baseRun);
      if (!base.checks.some((c) => c.checkId === 'drafted-hello' && c.exitCode !== null && c.exitCode !== 0)) throw new Error(`R16: the reproducing check did not fail on the base: ${JSON.stringify(base.checks)}`);
    });
    await withLine('r16-repro-plain-', async (rig) => {
      const r = await drafting(rig, { tests: (_, id) => bugTests(id) });
      awaiting(await admitAndDrive(r, { artifacts: artifactsFor(true, true) }), 'spec:1', 'a drafted spec with no template');
      const spec = lastAttempt(await rig.state(), 'spec');
      await approveAndResume(rig, r, 'spec:1', spec.sha256 ?? '');
      const [first] = (await rig.state()).drafts['test-design']?.attempts ?? [];
      const problems = first?.sentBack?.by === 'runtime' ? first.sentBack.problems : [];
      if (!problems.some((p) => p.code === 'not-applicable' && p.path.endsWith('#drafted-hello.reproduces'))) throw new Error(`I5: a reproduction drafted in a run that is not a bug fix went back for ${JSON.stringify(first?.sentBack)}`);
    });
  },
});

const DISMISSED_CANDIDATE_ADMITS_NO_RUN: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.dismissed-candidate-admits-no-run',
  title: 'POST /intake/:id/dismiss records a candidate\'s dismissal with a cause from the closed set and no text, beside the runs and under the candidate\'s template; a dismissed candidate admits no run, a second dismissal or one of an admitted candidate is refused, and a dismissal carrying text is refused',
  timeoutMs: 120_000,
  run: async () => {
    await withLine('r16-dismiss-', async (rig) => {
      const { admitRun, createApiServer } = await api();
      const policy = await bugFixPolicy();
      const candidate = await reportCandidate(rig, policy);
      const policyFile = join(rig.dirs.artifacts, '..', 'policy.yaml');
      await writeFile(policyFile, JSON.stringify(policy));
      const r = await drafting(rig);
      const server = createApiServer({ components: await r.components(), token: TOKEN, principal: 'conformance', policyFile, rolesDirectory: rig.roles, secrets: SECRETS });
      const address = await server.listen(0, '127.0.0.1');
      const dismiss = async (id: string, body: unknown): Promise<{ status: number; json: unknown }> => {
        const res = await fetch(`${address}/intake/${id}/dismiss`, { method: 'POST', body: JSON.stringify(body), headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${TOKEN}`, 'content-type': 'application/json' } });
        return { status: res.status, json: await res.json() };
      };
      try {
        const texted = await dismiss(candidate, { cause: 'duplicate', note: MARKER });
        if (texted.status !== 400) throw new Error(`R16: a dismissal carrying text answered ${String(texted.status)}`);
        const unknown = await dismiss(candidate, { cause: 'the reporter is wrong' });
        if (unknown.status !== 400) throw new Error(`R16: a dismissal with a free-text cause answered ${String(unknown.status)}`);
        const done = await dismiss(candidate, { cause: 'not-reproducible' });
        const summary = done.json as { dismissed?: { cause?: string } | null; template?: string };
        if (done.status !== 200 || summary.dismissed?.cause !== 'not-reproducible' || summary.template !== 'bug-fix') throw new Error(`R16: the dismissal answered ${String(done.status)} ${JSON.stringify(done.json)}`);
        if ((await dismiss(candidate, { cause: 'duplicate' })).status !== 409) throw new Error('R16: a second dismissal was not refused');
      } finally {
        await server.close();
      }
      const vault = await localVault(rig.dirs);
      const entry = await vault.readCandidate(candidate);
      if (entry?.dismissed?.cause !== 'not-reproducible' || entry.candidate?.template !== 'bug-fix' || entry.admittedAs !== null) throw new Error(`R16: the store holds ${JSON.stringify({ dismissed: entry?.dismissed, admittedAs: entry?.admittedAs })}`);
      if (JSON.stringify(entry.dismissed).includes(MARKER)) throw new Error('R16: the dismissal holds the report\'s text');
      if ((await vault.listRuns()).length !== 0) throw new Error('R16: a dismissal made a run');
      names(problemsOf(await admitRun(await bugRequest(r, { candidate, template: null })), 'a run from a dismissed candidate'), 'candidate', 'dismissed', 'a run from a dismissed candidate');
      // An admitted candidate cannot then be dismissed: the two spend one mark (D-R16-06).
      const other = await reportCandidateAgain(rig, policy);
      const admittedRun = await admitRun(await bugRequest(r, { candidate: other, template: null }));
      if (!admittedRun.ok) throw new Error(`R16: the run from a fresh candidate was refused as ${admittedRun.reason}: ${JSON.stringify(admittedRun)}`);
      if (await vault.dismissCandidate(other, 'duplicate', new Date().toISOString())) throw new Error('R16: an admitted candidate was dismissed');
    });
  },
});

/** A second report, delivered under another number, so its body and so its candidate differ from the first. */
async function reportCandidateAgain(rig: LineRig, policy: Policy): Promise<CandidateId> {
  const { Intake } = await api();
  const { signBody } = await triggers();
  const parsed = JSON.parse(reportBody().toString('utf8')) as { issue: { number: number } };
  parsed.issue.number = NUMBER - 1;
  const body = Buffer.from(JSON.stringify(parsed));
  const intake = new Intake({ vault: await localVault(rig.dirs), secrets: SECRETS });
  const outcome = await intake.deliver(policy, BUG_SUBSCRIPTION, signBody(body, SECRET), body);
  if (outcome.outcome !== 'candidate') throw new Error(`R16: the second report came to ${JSON.stringify(outcome)}`);
  return outcome.candidate;
}

const RUN_WITHOUT_TEMPLATE_AS_BEFORE: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.run-without-template-admits-as-before',
  title: 'a run with no template admits as it did before R16: its admission record holds no template, its intent record is the text, and its spec draft is the four files with no defect or reproduction part demanded',
  timeoutMs: 120_000,
  run: async () => {
    await withLine('r16-plain-', async (rig) => {
      const r = await drafting(rig);
      awaiting(await admitAndDrive(r, { policy: await draftingPolicy() }), 'spec:1', 'a drafted spec with no template');
      const record = await admitted(rig, r);
      if (record.template !== null) throw new Error(`R16: a run with no template was admitted with ${JSON.stringify(record.template)}`);
      const vault = (await r.components()).vault;
      if (record.intent === null) throw new Error('R16: the plain run recorded no intent');
      const intent = await readRecord<Record<string, unknown>>(vault, record.intent as never);
      if (typeof intent.text !== 'string' || 'template' in intent) throw new Error(`R16: the plain run's intent record is ${JSON.stringify(intent)}`);
      const attempt = lastAttempt(await rig.state(), 'spec');
      if (attempt.draft === null) throw new Error('R16: the plain run recorded no draft');
      const draft = await readRecord<DraftRecord>(vault, attempt.draft);
      if (draft.problems.length > 0 || draft.files.length !== 4 || draft.files.some((f) => f.path.endsWith('defect.json'))) throw new Error(`R16: the plain draft is ${JSON.stringify({ files: draft.files.map((f) => f.path), problems: draft.problems })}`);
    });
  },
});

 export async function drafting(rig: LineRig, seats: Seats = {}): Promise<DraftingRig> {

+  });

     const policy = overrides.policy ?? (await draftingPolicy());

     const drafted = [...(artifacts.spec === 'draft' ? ['spec' as const] : []), ...(artifacts.acceptanceTests === 'draft' ? ['test-design' as const] : [])];

     return rig.request(await components(), {

       rubrics,

           await writeFile(spec, '# hello\n\nThe capability, as locked.\n');
 
           const manifest = await vault.lock(runId, ['spec.md'], 'spec');
           const locked = entryFor(manifest.entries, 'spec.md');
 
           const intact = await vault.verifyLocks(runId);
           if (!intact.ok) {
             throw new Error(`I3: an untouched locked artifact verified as tampered (${JSON.stringify(intact.tampered)})`);

         const { StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(grantingDocument());
 
         const undefinedRole = engine.resolveCapabilities(UNDEFINED_ROLE, 'build', policy);
         if (undefinedRole.ok) {
           throw new Error('I4: a role the policy does not define resolved to a capability scope');
         }
         if (undefinedRole.reason !== 'capability-missing') {
           throw new Error(`I4: an undefined role was refused as '${undefinedRole.reason}'; expected 'capability-missing'`);
         }
 
         const forbidden = engine.resolveCapabilities(GRANTED_ROLE, FORBIDDEN_STATION, policy);
         if (forbidden.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I4: role '${GRANTED_ROLE}' resolved a scope at '${FORBIDDEN_STATION}', which its policy does not list`);
         }
         if (forbidden.reason !== 'station-forbidden') {
           throw new Error(`I4: a station outside the role scope was refused as '${forbidden.reason}'; expected 'station-forbidden'`);
         }
 
         // The control: an engine that refuses everything would satisfy both
         // checks above while granting nothing, so a granted station must
         // resolve, and to exactly what the document states.
         for (const station of GRANTED_STATIONS) {
           const granted = engine.resolveCapabilities(GRANTED_ROLE, station, policy);
           if (!granted.ok) {
             throw new Error(`I4: role '${GRANTED_ROLE}' was refused at '${station}', which its policy grants (${granted.reason})`);
           }
           if (granted.scope.tools.join(',') !== GRANTED_TOOLS.join(',')) {
             throw new Error(`I4: the resolved scope at '${station}' carries tools [${granted.scope.tools.join(', ')}]; expected [${GRANTED_TOOLS.join(', ')}]`);
           }
           if (granted.scope.tools.includes(UNGRANTED_TOOL)) {
             throw new Error(`I4: the resolved scope carries '${UNGRANTED_TOOL}', which no role in the document grants`);
           }
           if (granted.scope.network.egress !== 'none') {
             throw new Error('I4: the resolved scope widened egress beyond what the document granted');
           }
         }
 
         // A grant is not a handle on the policy: widening what came back must
         // not widen what the next caller receives.
         const held = engine.resolveCapabilities(GRANTED_ROLE, 'build', policy);
         if (!held.ok) throw new Error('I4: the control resolution was refused');
         try {
           held.scope.tools.push(UNGRANTED_TOOL);
         } catch {
           // Frozen, which is the intended arrangement.
         }
         const next = engine.resolveCapabilities(GRANTED_ROLE, 'build', policy);
         if (!next.ok) throw new Error('I4: the second resolution was refused');
         if (next.scope.tools.includes(UNGRANTED_TOOL)) {
           throw new Error(`I4: mutating a resolved scope added '${UNGRANTED_TOOL}' to what the policy grants`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I4.omitted-approval-is-human-required',
       title:
         'resolvePolicy fills every approval the document omits with human-required and never auto, while carrying a stated approval through unchanged, so the filling is a default rather than a rewrite',
       run: async () => {
         const { APPROVAL_KEYS, AUTONOMY_LEVELS, STATION_IDS, StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const document = grantingDocument();
         const stated = APPROVAL_KEYS.filter((key) => document.approvals[key] !== undefined);
         if (stated.length !== 1) {
           throw new Error(`I4: the fixture document must state exactly one approval for this assertion to mean anything; it states ${String(stated.length)}`);
         }
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(document);
 
         const expected = STATION_IDS.length * AUTONOMY_LEVELS.length;
         const keys = Object.keys(policy.approvals);
         if (keys.length !== expected) {
           throw new Error(`I4: the resolved policy carries ${String(keys.length)} approvals; the table must be total at ${String(expected)}`);
         }
         // Totality is proved by the count above; this loop is about what the
         // omitted keys were filled with.
         for (const key of APPROVAL_KEYS) {
           if (stated.includes(key)) continue;
           const outcome = policy.approvals[key];
           if (outcome !== 'human-required') {
             throw new Error(`I4: '${key}' was omitted by the document and resolved to '${outcome}'; an unconfigured approval must be human-required`);
           }
         }
         // The stated entry survives: a resolver that overwrote everything with
         // human-required would pass the loop above and be wrong.
         const [statedKey] = stated;
         if (statedKey === undefined) throw new Error('I4: the stated approval list has one entry and no entry in it');
         if (policy.approvals[statedKey] !== document.approvals[statedKey]) {
           throw new Error(`I4: the document stated '${statedKey}' and the resolved policy reports '${policy.approvals[statedKey]}'`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I4.empty-inventory-refuses-every-grant',
       title:
         'validateToolGrants against an empty inventory refuses every tool the policy grants, naming each with its role; an empty inventory is never read as allow-all',
       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine, validateToolGrants } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(grantingDocument());
 
         const empty = validateToolGrants(policy, []);
         if (empty.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I4: an empty inventory admitted ${String(GRANTED_TOOLS.length)} granted tool(s); an inventory offering nothing grants nothing`);
         }
         if (empty.ungranted.length !== GRANTED_TOOLS.length) {
           throw new Error(`I4: an empty inventory named ${String(empty.ungranted.length)} ungranted tool(s); expected all ${String(GRANTED_TOOLS.length)}`);
         }
         for (const tool of GRANTED_TOOLS) {
           if (!empty.ungranted.some((u) => u.tool === tool && u.role === GRANTED_ROLE)) {
             throw new Error(`I4: '${tool}' was granted to '${GRANTED_ROLE}' and the refusal does not name the pair`);
           }
         }
 
         // The control: a full inventory passes, so the refusal above is the
         // inventory doing work rather than the function refusing always.
         const full = validateToolGrants(policy, [...GRANTED_TOOLS]);
         if (!full.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I4: an inventory covering every grant was still refused (${full.detail})`);
         }
         // And one short of the grants refuses exactly the missing one.
         const [first] = GRANTED_TOOLS;
         if (first === undefined) throw new Error('I4: the granted tool list is empty');
         const partial = validateToolGrants(policy, [first]);
         if (partial.ok) throw new Error('I4: an inventory missing a granted tool was accepted');
         if (partial.ungranted.length !== GRANTED_TOOLS.length - 1) {
           throw new Error(`I4: a partial inventory named ${String(partial.ungranted.length)} ungranted tool(s); expected ${String(GRANTED_TOOLS.length - 1)}`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I4.egress-entry-names-one-host',
       title:
         'an egress host list carrying a wildcard, a path, a prefix length, or whitespace is refused; a list of literal hosts is accepted, so the refusal is the entry being read and not the list being rejected',
       run: async () => {
         const { validatePolicyDocument } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const document = grantingDocument();
         const scope = document.roles[GRANTED_ROLE];
         if (scope === undefined) throw new Error('I4: the fixture document defines no granted role');
 
         // Refusing the bare string 'all' while accepting ['*'] would leave the
         // authored form reading as an explicit allowlist while denoting every
         // host. Each of these denotes a set, or is not a host at all.
         for (const entry of ['*', '*.example.com', '0.0.0.0/0', '::/0', 'https://example.com', 'exa mple.com']) {
           const widened = { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, network: { egress: [entry] } } } };
           const outcome = validatePolicyDocument(widened);
           if (outcome.ok) {
             throw new Error(
               `I4: a policy granting egress to ${JSON.stringify(entry)} was accepted. An egress list admits no ` +
                 'wildcard, so an entry that denotes a set makes a restrictive-looking policy unrestricted.',
             );
           }
         }
 
         // The control: literal hosts are accepted, so the check above is the
         // entry being read rather than every list being refused.
         const literal = { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, network: { egress: ['registry.npmjs.org', '192.0.2.10'] } } } };
         const accepted = validatePolicyDocument(literal);
         if (!accepted.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I4: a list of literal hosts was refused: ${accepted.defects.map((d) => d.problem).join('; ')}`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I4.role-lookup-ignores-the-prototype-chain',
       title:
         'a role the policy does not define is refused with capability-missing even when Object.prototype carries a scope-shaped value under that name, and a role named for a prototype member refuses rather than throwing; the resolved role map has no prototype',
       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(grantingDocument());
         const prototype = Object.prototype as Record<string, unknown>;
 
         if (Object.getPrototypeOf(policy.roles) !== null) {
           throw new Error('I4: the resolved role map has a prototype, so an undefined role can be answered by it');
         }
 
         // Pollution somewhere else in the process must not become a grant here.
         prototype[UNDEFINED_ROLE] = { stations: ['build'], tools: ['read'] };
         try {
           const outcome = engine.resolveCapabilities(UNDEFINED_ROLE, 'build', policy);
           if (outcome.ok) {
             throw new Error(
               `I4: role '${UNDEFINED_ROLE}' is defined nowhere in the policy and resolved to a grant because ` +
                 'Object.prototype carried that name. Default deny has to be total, and a lookup that reads the ' +
                 'prototype chain is not.',
             );
           }
           if (outcome.reason !== 'capability-missing') {
             throw new Error(`I4: the polluted role was refused as '${outcome.reason}'; expected 'capability-missing'`);
           }
           if (engine.resolveAutonomy(1, 'build', UNDEFINED_ROLE, policy).ok) {
             throw new Error('I4: resolveAutonomy granted a level to a role that exists only on Object.prototype');
           }
         } finally {
           Reflect.deleteProperty(prototype, UNDEFINED_ROLE);
         }
 
         // A role named for a prototype member must refuse, not throw: an
         // exception is not the refusal the contract promises, and a caller that
         // catches it has no reason recorded.
         for (const name of ['__proto__', 'constructor', 'toString', 'valueOf']) {
           const role = name as typeof UNDEFINED_ROLE;
           let refused: string;
           try {
             const outcome = engine.resolveCapabilities(role, 'build', policy);
             refused = outcome.ok ? 'granted' : outcome.reason;
           } catch {
             throw new Error(`I4: an ungranted role named '${name}' threw instead of refusing with capability-missing`);
           }
           if (refused !== 'capability-missing') {
             throw new Error(`I4: an ungranted role named '${name}' resolved as '${refused}'`);
           }
         }
 
         // The control: a real grant still resolves.
         if (!engine.resolveCapabilities(GRANTED_ROLE, 'build', policy).ok) {
           throw new Error('I4: the granted role was refused, so the guard is refusing everything');
         }
       },
     }),
     APPROVAL_OUTCOME_GATES_THE_STATION,
     external({

             throw new Error(`I5: the refusal for L${String(level)} at '${station}' carries a level; there must be nothing on it to mistake for a grant`);
           }
           if (outcome.detail === '') {
             throw new Error(`I5: the refusal for L${String(level)} at '${station}' carries no detail`);
           }
         }
 
         // The global cap, which the fixture document sets to the maximum and
         // so can never bite on its own. Lowered here, it must refuse a request
         // the station cap and role ceiling would both have allowed.
         const grounded = engine.resolvePolicy({ ...grantingDocument(), globalCap: 0, stationCaps: {} });
         const global = engine.resolveAutonomy(1, 'verify', GRANTED_ROLE, grounded);
         if (global.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I5: L1 at 'verify' was granted at L${String(global.level)} under a global cap of L0`);
         }
         if (global.reason !== 'exceeds-cap') {
           throw new Error(`I5: a request above the global cap was refused as '${global.reason}'; expected 'exceeds-cap'`);
         }
         if (!global.detail.includes('global L0')) {
           throw new Error(`I5: the refusal does not name the bound that bit: ${global.detail}`);
         }
         if (!engine.resolveAutonomy(0, 'verify', GRANTED_ROLE, grounded).ok) {
           throw new Error('I5: L0 under a global cap of L0 was refused; the cap is a ceiling, not an exclusion');
         }
 
         // The control: a request within every bound is granted, and granted at
         // the level requested rather than raised or lowered to a cap. An engine
         // that refused everything would satisfy the loop above.
         for (const level of [0, 1] as const) {
           const outcome = engine.resolveAutonomy(level, 'build', GRANTED_ROLE, policy);
           if (!outcome.ok) {
             throw new Error(`I5: L${String(level)} at 'build' is within every bound and was refused (${outcome.reason}: ${outcome.detail})`);
           }
           if (outcome.level !== level) {
             throw new Error(`I5: L${String(level)} at 'build' resolved to L${String(outcome.level)}; a request inside the caps is granted as asked, never adjusted`);
           }
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I5.malformed-document-refused-at-resolve',
       title:
         'resolvePolicy refuses a value that does not validate as a PolicyDocument, even though the parameter type says it is one, so a caller that asserts past the compiler cannot resolve a grant out of a malformed document; a genuine document still resolves',
       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const document = grantingDocument();
         const scope = document.roles[GRANTED_ROLE];
         if (scope === undefined) throw new Error('I5: the fixture document defines no granted role');
 
         // Each of these is what a caller produces by writing `value as
         // PolicyDocument`. Without the guard, the first resolves a global cap
         // of 99 and grants every level; the rest widen a scope the document
         // never authorised.
         const rogues: Array<{ readonly what: string; readonly value: unknown }> = [
           { what: 'a global cap of 99', value: { ...document, globalCap: 99 } },
           { what: 'a role ceiling of 99', value: { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, autonomyCeiling: 99 } } } },
           { what: 'a station id that is not one of the ten', value: { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, stations: ['deploy'] } } } },
           { what: 'an unknown top-level key', value: { ...document, backdoor: true } },
         ];
         for (const { what, value } of rogues) {
           let refused = false;
           try {
             engine.resolvePolicy(value as never);
           } catch {
             refused = true;
           }
           if (!refused) {
             throw new Error(
               `I5: resolvePolicy accepted ${what}. The parameter type is a compiler claim, and a control that holds ` +
                 'only while every caller remembers to validate first is not closed.',
             );
           }
         }
 
         // The control: a genuine document resolves. A guard that threw on
         // everything would satisfy the loop above and break the engine.
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(document);
         if (policy.globalCap !== document.globalCap) {
           throw new Error(`I5: a valid document resolved to globalCap ${String(policy.globalCap)}, expected ${String(document.globalCap)}`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I5.malformed-autonomy-level-refused',
       title:
         'resolveAutonomy refuses a requested level that is not one of the four, rather than comparing it against the cap: NaN, a negative, and a fraction all survive a numeric comparison and would otherwise be answered with success carrying a level that is not a level',
       run: async () => {
         const { AUTONOMY_LEVELS, StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(grantingDocument());
 
         for (const [label, level] of [
           ['NaN', Number.NaN], ['-1', -1], ['2.5', 2.5], ['4', 4],
         ] as const) {
           let refused = false;
           try {
             const outcome = engine.resolveAutonomy(level as never, 'verify', GRANTED_ROLE, policy);
             if (outcome.ok) {
               throw new Error(
                 `I5: resolveAutonomy answered ${label} with success at level ${String(outcome.level)}. ` +
                   'An unsupported input is a refusal, and success is the one answer a caller must never get for a ' +
                   'level nobody can honour.',
               );
             }
           } catch (error) {
             if (error instanceof Error && error.message.startsWith('I5:')) throw error;
             refused = true;
           }
           if (!refused) throw new Error(`I5: resolveAutonomy returned a refusal rather than refusing ${label} outright`);
         }
 
         // The control: every real level is still answered normally.
         for (const level of AUTONOMY_LEVELS) {
           const outcome = engine.resolveAutonomy(level, 'verify', GRANTED_ROLE, policy);
           const allowed = level <= ROLE_CEILING;
           if (outcome.ok !== allowed) {
             throw new Error(`I5: level ${String(level)} at 'verify' was ${outcome.ok ? 'granted' : 'refused'}, expected the opposite`);
           }
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I5.sandbox-egress-allowlist-enforced',
       title:
         'a sandbox provisioned with egress mode allowlist reaches the one host it names and no other, by name and by address; ' +
         'the direct route does not exist, so unsetting every proxy variable reaches nothing; an empty allow list is refused rather ' +
         'than read as deny-all or allow-all; and deny-all is unchanged, with --network none, no proxy, and no network created',
       run: async () => {
         await withProvider('i5-egress-allowlist-', async (provider, dirs) => {
           // An allowlist that names nothing is refused. It is not deny-all wearing the wrong
           // name, and it is not permission to reach everything; either reading would be the
           // silent degrade this invariant exists to refuse (D-P2-07, reversed by P10).
           const empty = await refusalFrom(() => provider.provision(specFor(dirs, 'rw', { egress: { mode: 'allowlist', allow: [] } })));
           if (empty.layer !== 'egress') {
             throw new Error(`I5: an empty allowlist was refused at the ${empty.layer} layer, not the egress layer`);
           }
 
           // deny-all is untouched by the allowlist existing: no proxy is started beside a
           // sandbox entitled to no egress, and no network is created for it.
           await withSandbox(provider, specFor(dirs), async (handle) => {
             const controls = provider.appliedControls(handle);
             if (controls.egress.mode !== 'deny-all') {
               throw new Error(`I5: a deny-all spec was applied as ${controls.egress.mode}`);
             }
             // `network: 'none'` is the type's guarantee once the mode is deny-all, so what is
             // read here is what Docker was actually told and what the container actually got.
             if (!controls.runArgs.join(' ').includes('--network none')) {
               throw new Error(`I5: a deny-all sandbox was not started with --network none: ${controls.runArgs.join(' ')}`);
             }
             const interfaces = await provider.exec(handle, ['ls', '/sys/class/net']);
             if (interfaces.stdout.trim().split(/\s+/).join(',') !== 'lo') {
               throw new Error(`I5: a deny-all sandbox has interfaces [${interfaces.stdout.trim()}]; expected loopback alone`);
             }
           });
 
           const name = originName();
           const handle = await provider.provision(specFor(dirs, 'rw', { egress: { mode: 'allowlist', allow: [name] } }));
           const { egress } = allowlistControls(provider, handle);
           try {
             await withOrigin(egress.proxy.outboundNetwork, name, async (origin) => {
               // The grant. The body proves the origin answered, not the proxy on its behalf.
               const reached = await provider.exec(handle, [
                 'sh', '-c', `wget -T 8 -O - http://${origin.name}:${String(ORIGIN_PORT)}/ 2>&1`,
               ]);
               if (!reached.stdout.includes(ORIGIN_BODY)) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: the allowlisted host was not reached: ${reached.stdout}${reached.stderr}`);
               }
 
               // Refused by name, and by address. The second is the one that matters: a refusal
               // that only ever fires on a name is a DNS failure wearing an allowlist's clothes.
               for (const host of [BLOCKED_NAME, BLOCKED_ADDRESS]) {
                 const answer = await throughProxy(provider, handle, `GET http://${host}/ HTTP/1.0`);
                 if (!answer.includes('403') || !answer.includes(host)) {
                   throw new Error(`I5: ${host} is not on the allowlist and was not refused by name in the answer: ${answer}`);
                 }
               }
 
               // The strongest form: the origin's own address, which the proxy demonstrably
               // reaches, refused for the one reason under test — the address is not on the list.
               const sameHost = await throughProxy(provider, handle, `GET http://${origin.address}:${String(ORIGIN_PORT)}/ HTTP/1.0`);
               if (!sameHost.includes('403') || sameHost.includes(ORIGIN_BODY)) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: a reachable host was allowed by address although only its name was granted: ${sameHost}`);
               }
 
               // There is nothing to bypass. The internal network carries no default route, so a
               // process that unsets every proxy variable is left with no second path to take.
               const routes = await provider.exec(handle, ['sh', '-c', 'ip route']);
               if (routes.stdout.includes('default')) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: the sandbox has a default route, so the proxy is a convention rather than the only way out: ${routes.stdout}`);
               }
               const bypass = await provider.exec(handle, [
                 'sh', '-c',
                 'unset http_proxy https_proxy HTTP_PROXY HTTPS_PROXY no_proxy NO_PROXY; ' +
                   `wget -T 4 -O - http://${origin.address}:${String(ORIGIN_PORT)}/ 2>&1`,
               ]);
               if (bypass.exitCode === 0 || bypass.stdout.includes(ORIGIN_BODY)) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: the allowlisted host was reached with every proxy variable unset: ${bypass.stdout}`);
               }
             });
           } finally {
             await provider.destroy(handle);
           }
 
           // The proxy is the sandbox's, and goes with it. One left running is a route off the
           // host with no workload behind it and nobody watching it.
           for (const [kind, leaked] of [
             ['container', egress.proxy.name],
             ['network', egress.proxy.internalNetwork],
             ['network', egress.proxy.outboundNetwork],
           ] as const) {
             if (await dockerHas(kind, leaked)) throw new Error(`I5: the ${kind} ${leaked} outlived the sandbox it served`);
           }
         });
       },
     }),
     STATION_MISSING_CAPABILITY_REFUSED,
     TASK_ATTEMPTS_ARE_BOUNDED,
     OVER_REQUEST_REFUSED_AT_ADMISSION,
     UNSUPPORTED_STACK_IS_LOUD,
     // P11: a provider with no probe is named, never trusted to observe the product from inside it.
     HTTP_PROBE_ABSENT_IS_NAMED,
     ADAPTER_REFUSAL_ENFORCED_AT_ADMISSION,
     CHECK_COMMAND_HAS_A_GRAMMAR,
     MISSING_CHECK_OR_SHRUNKEN_SUITE_REFUSES,
     WORKSPACE_IS_WRITABLE_BY_THE_TASK,
     // P13: what the relay cannot count, it charges or refuses; it never forwards it free.
     MODEL_RELAY_FAILS_CLOSED_ON_UNMETERED_USAGE,
     // R6: the same, under the openai-chat dialect (A-R6-01).
     MODEL_RELAY_FAILS_CLOSED_ON_UNMETERED_USAGE_OPENAI_CHAT,
     MODEL_RELAY_FAILS_CLOSED_ON_UNMETERED_USAGE_OPENAI_RESPONSES,
     // R21: http and no credential are each a declaration, never an inference (A-R21-01, A-R21-02).
     MODEL_RELAY_REFUSES_AN_UNDECLARED_LOCAL_ENDPOINT,
     // I1a: what the walking skeleton's own declaration stood in for, each now refusing on its own.
     UNSAFE_DECLARATION_SURVIVES_COMPOSITION,
     ADAPTER_REFUSAL_REFUSES_L3_END_TO_END,
     UNANALYSED_TESTS_ESCALATE_THE_APPROVAL,
     external({
       id: 'I5.unanalysed-tests-are-not-reported-clean',
       title:
         'an L2 run on the composed host over a repository with no vitest or jest stops at the integrate approval for the unanalysed suite, which escalates an exit that would otherwise advance; paid, one build and one review call',
       level: 'runtime',
       package: '@olympus-ai/api',
       file: 'test/paid/line.paid.test.ts',
     }),
     // R15: no default declaration, nothing to reproduce is refused, and a check that did not start is not a failure.
     BASE_DECLARATION_HAS_NO_DEFAULT,

 /** Wraps a Vault so a test can act between two of its calls; every method forwards. */
 export function around(inner: Vault, hooks: { beforeLock?: (by: string) => Promise<void>; beforeCommit?: (s: RunState) => void; afterCommit?: (count: number) => void }): Vault {

     },
     verifyLocks: (runId) => inner.verifyLocks(runId),
     writeEvidence: (b) => inner.writeEvidence(b),

     recordUsage: (r) => inner.recordUsage(r),
     recordDecision: (d) => inner.recordDecision(d),
     readDecisions: (runId) => inner.readDecisions(runId),
     readUsage: (runId) => inner.readUsage(runId),
     recordIntegration: (r) => inner.recordIntegration(r),
     readIntegration: (runId) => inner.readIntegration(runId),
     recordBaseRun: (r) => inner.recordBaseRun(r),

     recordCriticResult: (r) => inner.recordCriticResult(r),

     listRuns: () => inner.listRuns(),
     recordTriggerDecision: (d, c) => inner.recordTriggerDecision(d, c),
     readTriggerDecisions: () => inner.readTriggerDecisions(),
     hasTriggerDecision: (source, key) => inner.hasTriggerDecision(source, key),
     readCandidate: (id) => inner.readCandidate(id),
     markCandidateAdmitted: (id, runId) => inner.markCandidateAdmitted(id, runId),
     markCandidateExpired: (id, at) => inner.markCandidateExpired(id, at),

     readRefusedDeliveryCounts: () => inner.readRefusedDeliveryCounts(),
     recordNightly: (r) => inner.recordNightly(r),

 /**

  * when present and wrong (the defect already reported).

@@ -685,86 +697,93 @@ function validateSchedule(value: unknown, path: string, defects: PolicyDefect[])
 /**
  * What the trigger policy means together (D-A-TF-03, the relational check
  * D-P3-13 left to admission until a registry existed). A non-human kind in
  * `enabled` needs its whole route; a subscription or schedule needs its kind
  * enabled; ids are unique. `human` keeps D-P3-05's route: an entry station,
  * a cap, and a floor, and no template.
  */
 function routeDefects(triggers: TriggerPolicy, path: string, defects: PolicyDefect[]): void {
   const nonHuman = triggers.enabled.filter((kind) => kind !== 'human');
   for (const kind of nonHuman) {
     const missing: string[] = [];
     if (!Object.hasOwn(triggers.entryStation, kind)) missing.push('an entryStation');
     if (!Object.hasOwn(triggers.taskTemplate, kind)) missing.push('a registered taskTemplate');
     if (!Object.hasOwn(triggers.maxAutonomy, kind)) missing.push('a maxAutonomy');
     if (!Object.hasOwn(triggers.minAuthorTrust, kind)) missing.push('a minAuthorTrust');
     if (!hasFieldSchema(kind)) missing.push('a field schema in code, which this kind does not have');
     if (missing.length > 0) {
       defects.push({ path: child(path, 'enabled'), problem: `'${kind}' is enabled without ${missing.join(', ')}; a non-human kind is enabled only with its whole route` });
     }
     // The one registered template records a candidate at intake, and a run admitted from it starts there; any
     // other station would validate and route nothing (codex-5). A template that enters elsewhere widens this.
     const station = Object.hasOwn(triggers.entryStation, kind) ? triggers.entryStation[kind] : undefined;
     if (station !== undefined && station !== 'intake') {
       defects.push({ path: child(child(path, 'entryStation'), kind), problem: `'${kind}' enters at '${station}', but every registered template enters at intake` });
     }
   }
   if (nonHuman.length > 0) {
     for (const key of ['maxDeliveryAgeMs', 'candidateExpiryMs'] as const) {
       if (triggers[key] === undefined) defects.push({ path: child(path, key), problem: 'required while a non-human kind is enabled' });
     }
   }
   const unique = (ids: readonly string[], at: string): void => {
     const seen = new Set<string>();
     for (const id of ids) {
       if (seen.has(id)) defects.push({ path: at, problem: `the id '${id}' appears more than once` });
       seen.add(id);
     }
   };
   const subscriptions = triggers.subscriptions ?? [];
   const schedules = triggers.schedules ?? [];
   unique(subscriptions.map((s) => s.id), child(path, 'subscriptions'));
   unique(schedules.map((s) => s.id), child(path, 'schedules'));
   subscriptions.forEach((s, i) => {
     if (!triggers.enabled.includes(s.kind)) {
       defects.push({ path: `${child(path, 'subscriptions')}[${String(i)}].kind`, problem: `'${s.kind}' is not enabled, so this subscription grants nothing` });
     }
   });
   if (schedules.length > 0 && !triggers.enabled.includes('scheduled')) {
     defects.push({ path: child(path, 'schedules'), problem: `'scheduled' is not enabled, so no schedule fires` });
   }
   // A job template runs a job in place of recording an event, so only a schedule's firing can name one (A-R13a-01).
   for (const [kind, template] of Object.entries(triggers.taskTemplate)) {
     if (kind !== 'scheduled' && isJobTemplate(template)) {
       defects.push({ path: child(child(path, 'taskTemplate'), kind), problem: `'${template}' is a job template, valid only on 'scheduled'` });
     }
     // A signal is attributed by the commit its event names, so only the two kinds that name one can carry it (D-A-OB-02).
     if (isSignalTemplate(template) && !SIGNAL_KINDS.some((k) => k === kind)) {
       defects.push({ path: child(child(path, 'taskTemplate'), kind), problem: `'${template}' is valid only on ${SIGNAL_KINDS.map((k) => `'${k}'`).join(' and ')}` });
     }
+    // A bug fix is drafted from a report a person read, and only a tracker's issue is one (A-R16-01).
+    if (isRunTemplate(template) && !BUG_FIX_KINDS.some((k) => k === kind)) {
+      defects.push({ path: child(child(path, 'taskTemplate'), kind), problem: `'${template}' is valid only on ${BUG_FIX_KINDS.map((k) => `'${k}'`).join(' and ')}` });
+    }
   }
   // An observe template with no window would never close one, or close it at once; neither is implied (I5, D-R8a-05).
   const observing = Object.values(triggers.taskTemplate).some(isObserveTemplate) || schedules.some((s) => isObserveTemplate(s.template));
   if (observing && triggers.observe === undefined) {
     defects.push({ path: child(path, 'observe'), problem: 'required while a template is observe-signal or observe-history; no window is implied' });
   }
   // Two walks of one branch would each record the same reversal and race to close the same window.
   // A walk closes windows; with no route for failed checks, a closed window would read as free of failures that were
   // never collected. So observing needs ci-failure enabled, sent to observe-signal, through a subscription (codex-8, I5).
   if (schedules.some((s) => s.template === 'observe-history')) {
     const routed = triggers.enabled.includes('ci-failure') && triggers.taskTemplate['ci-failure'] === 'observe-signal' && subscriptions.some((s) => s.kind === 'ci-failure');
     if (!routed) {
       defects.push({ path: child(path, 'schedules'), problem: "'observe-history' needs 'ci-failure' enabled, routed to 'observe-signal', through a subscription; without it no failed check is collected" });
     }
   }
   if (schedules.filter((s) => s.template === 'observe-history').length > 1) {
     defects.push({ path: child(path, 'schedules'), problem: `'observe-history' is named by more than one schedule; one walk keeps the record` });
   }
   schedules.forEach((s, i) => {
     if (isSignalTemplate(s.template)) {
       defects.push({ path: `${child(path, 'schedules')}[${String(i)}].template`, problem: `'${s.template}' records a delivery that names a commit; a schedule's firing names none` });
     }
+    if (isRunTemplate(s.template)) {
+      defects.push({ path: `${child(path, 'schedules')}[${String(i)}].template`, problem: `'${s.template}' records an issue someone reported; a schedule's firing is none` });
+    }
     if (s.template === 'nightly-security' && triggers.nightly === undefined) {
       defects.push({ path: `${child(path, 'schedules')}[${String(i)}].template`, problem: `'nightly-security' needs a nightly block naming its scanners and advisory host` });
     }
   });
 }

   deniesRule?: string;
+  /**
+   * That the check follows the spec's steps and so reproduces the defect a
+   * bug-fix run fixes (A-R16-04). Valid only with `onBase: 'fails'`, so R15's
+   * base run is what shows it fails; whether it follows the steps is the
+   * `test-design` critic's to judge. A bug-fix manifest needs a required one,
+   * and any other run's manifest may declare none.
+   */
+  reproduces?: true;
 }
 
 /** A unit check runs every suite the tree's test framework is configured to run. */

 /** Reported as `actual` for a locked path that no longer exists: a deleted artifact is a mismatch, not an empty file (D-S1-02). */
 const MISSING = 'missing';
 
@@ -786,598 +789,616 @@ async function readJsonOrNull(file: string): Promise<unknown> {
 export class LocalVault implements Vault {
   readonly #store: string;
   readonly #artifacts: string;
   /** Each refused-delivery count file's pending write, so this process never loses its own increment. */
   readonly #counting = new Map<string, Promise<void>>();
 
   constructor(roots: VaultRoots) {
     const store = resolve(roots.store);
     const artifacts = resolve(roots.artifacts);
     if (contains(store, artifacts) || contains(artifacts, store)) {
       throw new Error(
         `LocalVault: the store (${store}) and the artifact root (${artifacts}) overlap. The artifact root is the tree ` +
           'an agent writes; a store inside it, or holding it, is a Vault an agent can reach, which is exactly what I1 denies.',
       );
     }
     this.#store = store;
     this.#artifacts = artifacts;
   }
 
   async read(ref: VaultRef): Promise<Uint8Array> {
     let bytes: Uint8Array;
     try {
       bytes = await readFile(objectFile(this.#store, ref));
     } catch (error) {
       if (!isAbsent(error)) throw error;
       // A base record written before R19 lives only under its fixed name.
       const legacy = ref.kind === 'base-run' ? await readFile(join(runDir(this.#store, ref.runId), BASE_RUN_FILE)).catch(() => undefined) : undefined;
       if (legacy === undefined) throw new Error(`LocalVault: no ${ref.kind} ${ref.hash} for run ${ref.runId}`, { cause: error });
       bytes = legacy;
     }
     // Every other kind is named by its hash, so the file a ref names holds that
     // content. The admission record is named by its run, so a ref carrying some
     // other hash must not read it back as if it matched.
     if ((ref.kind === 'admission' || ref.kind === 'base-run') && sha256(bytes) !== ref.hash) {
       throw new Error(`LocalVault: no ${ref.kind} ${ref.hash} for run ${ref.runId}; the run's ${ref.kind} record hashes to ${sha256(bytes)}`);
     }
     return bytes;
   }
 
   /**
    * Adds to the run's manifest; never replaces it. `spec` locks the spec and
    * `test-design` locks the acceptance tests, and I3 requires both still
    * verified at every later transition, so a later lock that dropped the
    * earlier one would make the invariant unsatisfiable. An already-locked path
    * is refused rather than re-hashed: re-locking is how a tamper would launder
    * itself, and the station and instant of the first lock are the audit record.
    */
   async lock(runId: RunId, paths: string[], by: StationId): Promise<LockManifest> {
     if (paths.length === 0) {
       throw new Error(`LocalVault: refusing to lock nothing for run ${runId}; an empty manifest would verify as intact`);
     }
     refuseUnusablePaths(runId, paths);
 
     const locks = join(runDir(this.#store, runId), 'locks');
     const generation = await highestIn(locks);
     const existing: LockEntry[] =
       generation === null ? [] : [...requireManifest(await readJson(join(locks, `${String(generation)}.json`)), `${String(generation)}.json`).entries];
 
     const already = paths.filter((path) => existing.some((entry) => entry.path === path));
     if (already.length > 0) {
       const held = already.map((path) => {
         const entry = existing.find((e) => e.path === path);
         return `'${path}' (locked at ${entry === undefined ? 'an earlier station' : entry.lockedBy})`;
       });
       throw new Error(
         `LocalVault: refusing to lock ${held.join(', ')} again for run ${runId}. An artifact is fixed from its first ` +
           'lock; re-locking would rebase its hash onto whatever the bytes are now.',
       );
     }
 
     const root = await artifactRoot(this.#artifacts);
     const lockedAt = new Date().toISOString();
     const added: LockEntry[] = [];
     for (const path of paths) {
       const hash = await hashArtifact(root, path);
       if (hash === MISSING) throw new Error(`LocalVault: cannot lock ${path} for run ${runId}: no such file under ${this.#artifacts}`);
       if (hash === ESCAPED) throw new Error(`LocalVault: cannot lock ${path} for run ${runId}: it resolves outside ${this.#artifacts}`);
       if (hash === UNREADABLE) throw new Error(`LocalVault: cannot lock ${path} for run ${runId}: it exists but cannot be read as bytes`);
       added.push({ path, sha256: hash, lockedAt, lockedBy: by });
     }
 
     const manifest: LockManifest = { runId, entries: [...existing, ...added] };
     const next = (generation ?? 0) + 1;
     if (!(await createExclusive(join(locks, `${String(next)}.json`), encode(manifest)))) {
       throw new Error(
         `LocalVault: another writer locked generation ${String(next)} for run ${runId} first; nothing was stored. ` +
           'Re-read the manifest and lock again.',
       );
     }
     return manifest;
   }
 
   async verifyLocks(runId: RunId): Promise<LockVerdict> {
     const locks = join(runDir(this.#store, runId), 'locks');
     const generation = await highestIn(locks);
     if (generation === null) {
       throw new Error(`LocalVault: nothing is locked for run ${runId}; refusing to report intact`);
     }
     const file = join(locks, `${String(generation)}.json`);
     const manifest = requireManifest(await readJson(file), file);
 
     const root = await artifactRoot(this.#artifacts);
     const tampered: Array<{ path: string; expected: string; actual: string }> = [];
     for (const entry of manifest.entries) {
       const actual = await hashArtifact(root, entry.path);
       if (actual !== entry.sha256) tampered.push({ path: entry.path, expected: entry.sha256, actual });
     }
     return tampered.length === 0 ? { ok: true } : { ok: false, tampered };
   }
 
   writeEvidence(b: EvidenceBundle): Promise<VaultRef> {
     return storeObject(this.#store, b.runId, 'evidence', b);
   }
 
   recordViolation(v: IntegrityViolation): Promise<VaultRef> {
     return storeObject(this.#store, v.runId, 'violation', v);
   }
 
   /**
    * Once per run. The record is created with the same exclusive create as run
    * state, under a fixed name rather than its hash, so a second admission for
    * the run finds the file present and stores nothing: a run cannot be
    * re-admitted at another level or under another policy.
    */
   async recordAdmission(a: AdmissionRecord): Promise<VaultRef> {
     if (!isRecord(a) || !isRecord(a.run) || typeof a.run.id !== 'string') {
       throw new Error('LocalVault: an admission record must carry the run it admits');
     }
     // A stack not written at admission cannot be backfilled, so a record without one is refused (R18).
     if (typeof (a.stack as unknown) !== 'string' || a.stack === '') {
       throw new Error(`LocalVault: the admission record of run ${a.run.id} names no stack; nothing was stored`);
     }
     // The roles a run's seats run under cannot be supplied later, so a record without them is refused (D-A-RC-01).
     const roles = rolesProblem(a.roles);
     if (roles !== undefined) throw new Error(`LocalVault: the admission record of run ${a.run.id} does not pin its roles: ${roles}; nothing was stored`);
     // What a run's seats are given of the target's instructions is fixed here, so a set that does not verify is refused (A-R24-03).
     const instructions = repoInstructionsProblem(a.repoInstructions);
     if (instructions !== undefined) throw new Error(`LocalVault: the admission record of run ${a.run.id}: ${instructions}; nothing was stored`);
     const runId = a.run.id;
     const bytes = encode(a);
     if (!(await createExclusive(join(runDir(this.#store, runId), ADMISSION_FILE), bytes))) {
       throw new Error(`LocalVault: run ${runId} already has an admission record; a run is admitted once, and nothing was stored`);
     }
     return { runId, kind: 'admission', hash: sha256(bytes) };
   }
 
   recordTaskResult(runId: RunId, r: TaskResult): Promise<VaultRef> {
     if (!isRecord(r) || typeof r.taskId !== 'string') {
       return Promise.reject(new Error(`LocalVault: refusing to record a task result for run ${runId} that names no task`));
     }
     return storeObject(this.#store, runId, 'task-result', r);
   }
 
   /**
    * I2: only a record the runtime collected. A reading is checked for its
    * shape too, because a total derived from a record with a missing count
    * would be a figure nobody measured.
    */
   recordUsage(r: UsageRecord): Promise<VaultRef> {
     const problem = usageProblem(r);
     if (problem !== undefined) return Promise.reject(new Error(`LocalVault: refusing a usage record: ${problem}`));
     return storeObject(this.#store, r.runId, 'usage', r);
   }
 
   /**

   recordDecision(d: EnforcementDecision): Promise<VaultRef> {
     const problem = decisionProblem(d);
     if (problem !== undefined) return Promise.reject(new Error(`LocalVault: refusing an enforcement decision: ${problem}`));

   }
 
   /** Every decision file under the run, by its name; the name is the hash, so each ref reads back the file it names. */
   readDecisions(runId: RunId): Promise<readonly VaultRef[]> {

   }
 
   /** Every usage file under the run, referenced from run state or not (A-I1a-05). */
   readUsage(runId: RunId): Promise<readonly VaultRef[]> {
     return this.#objects(runId, 'usage');
   }
 
   /**
    * I2: only the runtime's record, whole. Once per run and draft, by an
    * exclusive create: a second base run would let a run or a draft be judged
    * against whichever base suited it, so it is refused and nothing more is
    * stored. The record is stored under its hash first, so the name that makes
    * it once-only never points at a record that is not there (R19).
    */
   async recordBaseRun(r: BaseRunRecord): Promise<VaultRef> {
     const problem = baseRunProblem(r);
     if (problem !== undefined) throw new Error(`LocalVault: refusing a base record: ${problem}`);
     const bytes = encode(r);
     const ref: VaultRef = { runId: r.runId, kind: 'base-run', hash: sha256(bytes) };
     const named = join(runDir(this.#store, r.runId), baseRunFile(r.draft));
     if (await pathExists(named)) {
       throw new Error(`LocalVault: run ${r.runId} already has a base record${r.draft === null ? '' : ` for draft ${r.draft}`}; the checks run over a base once, and nothing was stored`);
     }
     await createExclusive(objectFile(this.#store, ref), bytes);
     if (!(await createExclusive(named, bytes))) {
       throw new Error(`LocalVault: run ${r.runId} already has a base record${r.draft === null ? '' : ` for draft ${r.draft}`}; the checks run over a base once, and nothing was stored`);
     }
     return ref;
   }
 
   async readBaseRun(runId: RunId, draft: string | null): Promise<VaultRef | null> {
     if (draft !== null && !SHA256_HEX.test(draft)) throw new Error(`LocalVault: '${draft}' is not a draft hash`);
     let bytes: Uint8Array;
     try {
       bytes = await readFile(join(runDir(this.#store, runId), baseRunFile(draft)));
     } catch (error) {
       if (isAbsent(error)) return null;
       throw error;
     }
     return { runId, kind: 'base-run', hash: sha256(bytes) };
   }
 
   /** I7: only a person's intent, as the principal's channel supplied it (R19). */
   recordIntent(r: IntentRecord): Promise<VaultRef> {

+    if (!isRecord(r) || !hasString(r, 'runId')) return Promise.reject(new Error('LocalVault: refusing an intent that names no run'));

   recordRubric(r: RubricRecord): Promise<VaultRef> {
     if (!isRecord(r) || !hasString(r, 'runId') || !hasString(r, 'text') || typeof r.station !== 'string' || !DRAFT_STATIONS.has(r.station)) {

   }
 
   recordDraft(r: DraftRecord): Promise<VaultRef> {
     const problem = draftProblem(r);

   recordCriticResult(r: CriticRecord): Promise<VaultRef> {

   /**
    * Once per run and station, by an exclusive create: an approved draft is
    * locked once (R19). The same record again returns its ref, so a resume
    * after a stop that fell between this and the state's commit can finish
    * (A-R19-11); any other record at that station is refused.
    */
   async recordDraftLock(r: DraftLockRecord): Promise<VaultRef> {
     const problem = draftLockProblem(r);
     if (problem !== undefined) throw new Error(`LocalVault: refusing a draft lock: ${problem}`);
     const bytes = encode(r);
     const ref: VaultRef = { runId: r.runId, kind: 'draft-lock', hash: sha256(bytes) };
     await createExclusive(objectFile(this.#store, ref), bytes);
     const station = join(runDir(this.#store, r.runId), draftLockFile(r.station));
     if (!(await createExclusive(station, bytes)) && sha256(await readFile(station)) !== ref.hash) {
       throw new Error(`LocalVault: run ${r.runId} already locked another draft at ${r.station}; nothing more was stored`);
     }
     return ref;
   }
 
   async readLocks(runId: RunId): Promise<LockManifest | null> {
     const locks = join(runDir(this.#store, runId), 'locks');
     const generation = await highestIn(locks);
     if (generation === null) return null;
     const file = join(locks, `${String(generation)}.json`);
     return requireManifest(await readJson(file), file);
   }
 
   /** I2: only what the runtime did, whole. The same act recorded twice is one entry, since the record carries no time. */
   recordIntegration(r: IntegrationRecord): Promise<VaultRef> {
     const problem = integrationProblem(r);
     if (problem !== undefined) return Promise.reject(new Error(`LocalVault: refusing an integration record: ${problem}`));
     return storeObject(this.#store, r.runId, 'integration', r);
   }
 
   readIntegration(runId: RunId): Promise<readonly VaultRef[]> {

   /** Every run directory under the store, whatever it holds, so a run refused at admission is listed too (A-R2-02). */
   async listRuns(): Promise<readonly RunId[]> {
     let entries: Dirent[];
     try {
       entries = await readdir(join(this.#store, 'runs'), { withFileTypes: true });
     } catch (error) {
       if (isAbsent(error)) return [];
       throw error;
     }
     return entries
       .filter((entry) => entry.isDirectory() && RUN_ID.test(entry.name))
       .map((entry) => entry.name as RunId)
       .sort();
   }
 
 
   /**
    * One exclusive create under `intake/decisions/<id>.json` holds the
    * decision and its candidate, so the replay key and the outcome are one
    * write (D-R22-02): a failed write leaves no key, and a second one loses
    * the race to `EEXIST`. Kept under `intake/`, beside `runs/`, so
    * `listRuns` never names one (D-R22-03).
    */
   async recordTriggerDecision(d: TriggerDecision, candidate: CandidateRecord | null): Promise<boolean> {
     const problem = intakeRecordProblem(d, candidate);
     if (problem !== undefined) throw new Error(`LocalVault: ${problem}`);
     return createExclusive(join(this.#store, 'intake', 'decisions', `${intakeIdOf(d.source, d.key)}.json`), encode({ decision: d, candidate }));
   }
 
 
   async hasTriggerDecision(source: string, key: string): Promise<boolean> {
     return pathExists(join(this.#store, 'intake', 'decisions', `${intakeIdOf(source, key)}.json`));
   }
   async readTriggerDecisions(): Promise<readonly IntakeEntry[]> {
     let names: string[];
     try {
       names = await readdir(join(this.#store, 'intake', 'decisions'));
     } catch (error) {
       if (isAbsent(error)) return [];
       throw error;
     }
     const entries: IntakeEntry[] = [];
     for (const name of names) {
       const id = /^([0-9a-f]{64})\.json$/.exec(name)?.[1];
       if (id === undefined) continue;
       const entry = await this.#intakeEntry(id as CandidateId);
       if (entry !== null) entries.push(entry);
     }
     return entries.sort((a, b) => (a.decision.source === b.decision.source ? a.decision.key.localeCompare(b.decision.key) : a.decision.source.localeCompare(b.decision.source)));
   }
 
   async readCandidate(id: CandidateId): Promise<IntakeEntry | null> {
     if (!/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/.test(id)) return null;
     const entry = await this.#intakeEntry(id);
     return entry?.candidate == null ? null : entry;
   }
 
   async markCandidateAdmitted(id: CandidateId, runId: RunId): Promise<boolean> {
     if ((await this.readCandidate(id)) === null) throw new Error(`LocalVault: no candidate ${id}`);
     return createExclusive(join(this.#store, 'intake', 'admitted', `${id}.json`), encode({ runId: requireRunId(runId) }));
   }
 
+  /** The same exclusive mark an admission takes, so a dismissal and an admission cannot both land (D-R16-06). */
+  async dismissCandidate(id: CandidateId, cause: DismissalCause, at: string): Promise<boolean> {
+    if ((await this.readCandidate(id)) === null) throw new Error(`LocalVault: no candidate ${id}`);
+    if (!LOCAL_DISMISSAL_CAUSES.has(cause)) throw new Error(`LocalVault: '${cause}' is not a dismissal cause`);
+    if (Number.isNaN(Date.parse(at))) throw new Error(`LocalVault: '${at}' is not a time`);
+    return createExclusive(join(this.#store, 'intake', 'admitted', `${id}.json`), encode({ dismissed: { cause, at } }));

   async markCandidateExpired(id: CandidateId, at: string): Promise<void> {
     if ((await this.readCandidate(id)) === null) throw new Error(`LocalVault: no candidate ${id}`);
     // A second call loses the exclusive create and keeps the first time.
     await createExclusive(join(this.#store, 'intake', 'expired', `${id}.json`), encode({ at }));
   }
 
   /**
    * One file per bucket and hour, rewritten under a per-file chain in this
    * process. Two processes counting into one store can lose an increment; a
    * count is a rate for the nightly review, not a control, and a lost one
    * understates junk traffic rather than admitting any (D-R22-03).
    */
   async countRefusedDelivery(bucket: string, hour: string, cause: CountedRefusalCause): Promise<void> {
     if (!/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}T\d{2}$/.test(hour)) throw new Error(`LocalVault: '${hour}' is not a UTC hour`);
     const file = join(this.#store, 'intake', 'counts', hour, `${sha256(new TextEncoder().encode(bucket))}.json`);
     const previous = this.#counting.get(file) ?? Promise.resolve();
     const next = previous.then(async () => {
       const read = await readJsonOrNull(file);
       const counts: Record<string, number> = isRecord(read) && isRecord(read.counts) ? { ...(read.counts as Record<string, number>) } : {};
       counts[cause] = (counts[cause] ?? 0) + 1;
       await mkdir(dirname(file), { recursive: true });
       await writeFile(file, encode({ bucket, hour, counts }));
     });
     this.#counting.set(file, next.catch(() => undefined));
     await next;
   }
 
   async readRefusedDeliveryCounts(): Promise<readonly RefusedDeliveryCount[]> {
     const root = join(this.#store, 'intake', 'counts');
     let hours: string[];
     try {
       hours = await readdir(root);
     } catch (error) {
       if (isAbsent(error)) return [];
       throw error;
     }
     const out: RefusedDeliveryCount[] = [];
     for (const hour of hours.sort()) {
       const rows: RefusedDeliveryCount[] = [];
       for (const name of await readdir(join(root, hour))) {
         const read = await readJson(join(root, hour, name));
         if (!isRecord(read) || typeof read.bucket !== 'string' || !isRecord(read.counts)) throw new Error(`LocalVault: ${join(root, hour, name)} is not a refused-delivery count`);
         rows.push({ bucket: read.bucket, hour, counts: read.counts });
       }
       out.push(...rows.sort((a, b) => a.bucket.localeCompare(b.bucket)));
     }
     return out;
   }
 
   /** Under `nightly/<sha256>.json`, beside `runs/` and `intake/`, so `listRuns` never names one (A-R13a-02). */
   async recordNightly(r: NightlyRecord): Promise<string> {
     if (!isRecord(r) || (r.collectedBy as string) !== 'runtime') throw new Error("LocalVault: a nightly record is collected by the runtime");
     const bytes = encode(r);
     const hash = sha256(bytes);
     await createExclusive(join(this.#store, NIGHTLY_DIR, `${hash}.json`), bytes);
     return hash;
   }
 
   async readNightly(): Promise<readonly NightlyEntry[]> {
     const dir = join(this.#store, NIGHTLY_DIR);
     let names: string[];
     try {
       names = await readdir(dir);
     } catch (error) {
       if (isAbsent(error)) return [];
       throw error;
     }
     const out: NightlyEntry[] = [];
     for (const name of names) {
       const hash = CONTENT_NAME.exec(name)?.[1];
       if (hash === undefined) continue;
       const bytes = await readFile(join(dir, name));
       const record = JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(bytes)) as unknown;
       if (!isRecord(record) || record.collectedBy !== 'runtime') throw new Error(`LocalVault: ${join(dir, name)} is not a nightly record`);
       // The hash is the name's, not recomputed: a drifted record is the re-hash's to find, not this reader's to hide.
       // A record stored before R13b has no review, and one before R8a no observation; each is read as none.
       out.push({ record: { review: null, observation: null, ...record } as unknown as NightlyRecord, sha256: hash });
     }
     return out.sort((a, b) => a.record.date.localeCompare(b.record.date) || a.record.schedule.localeCompare(b.record.schedule) || a.sha256.localeCompare(b.sha256));
   }
 
   /** Under `runs/<id>/observations/<sha256>.json`, or `observations/<sha256>.json` beside the runs (D-R8a-03). */
   async recordObservation(r: ObservationRecord): Promise<string> {
     const problem = observationProblem(r);
     if (problem !== undefined) throw new Error(`LocalVault: refusing an observation: ${problem}`);
     const dir = r.runId === null ? join(this.#store, OBSERVATIONS_DIR) : join(runDir(this.#store, r.runId), OBSERVATIONS_DIR);
     const bytes = encode(r);
     const hash = sha256(bytes);
     await createExclusive(join(dir, `${hash}.json`), bytes);
     return hash;
   }
 
   async readObservations(runId: RunId | null): Promise<readonly ObservationEntry[]> {
     const dir = runId === null ? join(this.#store, OBSERVATIONS_DIR) : join(runDir(this.#store, runId), OBSERVATIONS_DIR);
     let names: string[];
     try {
       names = await readdir(dir);
     } catch (error) {
       if (isAbsent(error)) return [];
       throw error;
     }
     const out: ObservationEntry[] = [];
     for (const name of names) {
       const hash = CONTENT_NAME.exec(name)?.[1];
       if (hash === undefined) continue;
       const record = await readJson(join(dir, name));
       // A record that is not an observation is a fault, never a record to skip (I5); a drifted hash is the re-hash's to find.
       const problem = observationProblem(record);
       if (problem !== undefined) throw new Error(`LocalVault: ${join(dir, name)} is not an observation: ${problem}`);
       if ((record as ObservationRecord).runId !== runId) throw new Error(`LocalVault: ${join(dir, name)} names another run`);
       out.push({ record: record as ObservationRecord, sha256: hash });
     }
     return out.sort((a, b) => a.record.recordedAt.localeCompare(b.record.recordedAt) || a.sha256.localeCompare(b.sha256));
   }
 
   /**
    * Walks the whole store, sorted, and yields every file in it. A file under
    * `runs/<id>/objects/<kind>/` or `nightly/` is named by its hash; every
    * other is named by a run, a number, or an id. An entry that is neither a
    * file nor a directory is not a record this store writes, and stops the
    * walk rather than being skipped (I5).
    */
   async *listObjects(): AsyncIterable<StoredObject> {
     const store = this.#store;
     async function* walk(dir: string, rel: readonly string[]): AsyncGenerator<StoredObject> {
       let entries: Dirent[];
       try {
         entries = await readdir(dir, { withFileTypes: true });
       } catch (error) {
         if (isAbsent(error) && rel.length === 0) return;
         throw error;
       }
       entries.sort((a, b) => (a.name < b.name ? -1 : a.name > b.name ? 1 : 0));
       for (const entry of entries) {
         const parts = [...rel, entry.name];
         const full = join(dir, entry.name);
         if (entry.isDirectory()) {
           yield* walk(full, parts);
           continue;
         }
         if (!entry.isFile()) throw new Error(`LocalVault: ${parts.join('/')} in the store is neither a file nor a directory`);
         yield { path: parts.join('/'), runId: runOf(parts), named: contentNamed(parts) ? 'content' : 'name', bytes: await readFile(full) };
       }
     }
     yield* walk(store, []);
   }
 
   async #intakeEntry(id: CandidateId): Promise<IntakeEntry | null> {
     const file = join(this.#store, 'intake', 'decisions', `${id}.json`);
     let bytes: Uint8Array;
     try {
       bytes = await readFile(file);
     } catch (error) {
       if (isAbsent(error)) return null;
       throw error;
     }
     const parsed: unknown = JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(bytes));
     if (!isRecord(parsed) || !isRecord(parsed.decision) || intakeIdOf(String(parsed.decision.source), String(parsed.decision.key)) !== id) {
       throw new Error(`LocalVault: ${file} is not the trigger decision its name says`);
     }
     const admitted = await readJsonOrNull(join(this.#store, 'intake', 'admitted', `${id}.json`));
     const expired = await readJsonOrNull(join(this.#store, 'intake', 'expired', `${id}.json`));
     const stored = (parsed.candidate ?? null) as (Omit<CandidateRecord, 'schema'> & { schema?: CandidateRecord['schema'] }) | null;
     return {
       decision: parsed.decision as unknown as TriggerDecision,
       // A candidate stored before R13a names no schema: its fields were checked against its kind's (A-R13a-07).
       candidate: stored === null ? null : { ...stored, schema: stored.schema ?? stored.kind },
       sha256: sha256(bytes),
       admittedAs: isRecord(admitted) && typeof admitted.runId === 'string' ? (admitted.runId as RunId) : null,
       expiredAt: isRecord(expired) && typeof expired.at === 'string' ? expired.at : null,
+      // The spend mark holds a run or a dismissal, never both (D-R16-06).
+      dismissed: isRecord(admitted) && isRecord(admitted.dismissed) && typeof admitted.dismissed.cause === 'string' && typeof admitted.dismissed.at === 'string'
+        ? { cause: admitted.dismissed.cause as DismissalCause, at: admitted.dismissed.at }
+        : null,
     };
   }
   async #objects(runId: RunId, kind: 'decision' | 'usage' | 'integration'): Promise<readonly VaultRef[]> {
     const dir = join(runDir(this.#store, runId), 'objects', kind);
     let names: string[];
     try {
       names = await readdir(dir);
     } catch (error) {
       if (isAbsent(error)) return [];
       throw error;
     }
     return names
       .map((name) => /^([0-9a-f]{64})\.json$/.exec(name)?.[1])
       .filter((hash): hash is string => hash !== undefined)
       .sort()
       .map((hash) => ({ runId, kind, hash }));
   }
 
   async readRunState(runId: RunId): Promise<RunState> {
     const states = join(runDir(this.#store, runId), 'state');
     const version = await highestIn(states);
     if (version === null) throw Object.assign(new Error(`LocalVault: no run state for ${runId}`), { code: 'no-run-state' as const, runId }) satisfies NoRunState;
     const file = join(states, `${String(version)}.json`);
     return requireRunState(await readJson(file), file);
   }
 
   /**
    * Compare-and-swap, arbitrated by the filesystem. The next version's file is
    * created with `wx`, so of any number of writers holding the same
    * `ifVersion` exactly one creates it and the rest are refused with `EEXIST`.
    * There is deliberately no read-then-compare here: that shape loses an
    * update whenever two writers interleave between the read and the write, and
    * it passes a sequential test while doing so.
    *
    * `ifVersion` is authoritative and `s.version` is ignored; the contract left
    * the choice to this unit (D-S1, S1 spec section 2). The precondition below
    * is an extra refusal, not the concurrency control: it rejects a version
    * that never existed, so a caller cannot jump the run forward and leave a
    * hole in its history.
    */
   async commitRunState(s: RunState, ifVersion: string): Promise<RunState> {
     if (!VERSION.test(ifVersion)) {
       throw new Error(`LocalVault: '${ifVersion}' is not a run state version`);
     }
     const states = join(runDir(this.#store, s.runId), 'state');
     const current = Number(ifVersion);
     if (!Number.isSafeInteger(current)) throw new Error(`LocalVault: run state version ${ifVersion} is out of range`);
 
     const held = await highestIn(states);
     if (current === 0 ? held !== null : held === null || held < current) {
       const at = held === null ? 'no committed state' : `version ${String(held)}`;
       throw new Error(`LocalVault: run ${s.runId} has ${at}, so version ${ifVersion} was never current; nothing stored`);
     }
 
     // The history it replaces, read before anything is written: a commit may extend it and never shorten or rewrite it (codex-5).
     const previous = current === 0 ? [] : requireRunState(await readJson(join(states, `${ifVersion}.json`)), `${ifVersion}.json`).stations;
     const problem = stationsProblem(previous, (s as { stations?: unknown }).stations);
     if (problem !== undefined) throw new Error(`LocalVault: refusing run ${s.runId}'s state: ${problem}; nothing stored`);
 
     const next = String(current + 1);
     const stored: RunState = { ...s, version: next };
     if (!(await createExclusive(join(states, `${next}.json`), encode(stored)))) {
       throw new Error(
         `LocalVault: run ${s.runId} has already moved past version ${ifVersion}; another writer committed ${next} first. ` +
           'Nothing was stored. Re-read the state and commit again.',
       );
     }
     return stored;
   }
 }
 
 /**
  * The one check every reader of an admission record's repository
  * instructions makes (A-R24-03): each file names a path in the fixed list,
  * once, and its hash is the hash of its text, so the record holds exactly the
  * versions it names and a resume delivers what admission read (I3, I5).
  */
 
 /** A path the reader can list: the two root files, a rule, or a skill's `SKILL.md`. */
